- 🚌 **IF THE OWNER IS UNREACHABLE — single-owner custody is the one real blocker** (owner, 2026-09-18 10:2x: *"is any of this a blocker to go live or would be a blocker if i was unreachable for an extended period of time"*). Measured 10:2x: the GitHub repo has ONE collaborator (`skymitch9`), the Fly org is his PERSONAL org (no second member), `TEST_MODE` is a Fly secret only he flips (cutover P5, owner-only by standing rule), deploys run only from his machine's flyctl login, the Google Cloud project / Discord developer portal / 1Password vault *Black Bloc* / the `heygabi.ai` DNS are all his. Consequence: with him away, staff can still run the bot and change every keyed setting on the site, but NOBODY can deploy, lift test mode, rotate a secret, or pay Fly. Nothing in the strings audit is a blocker either way. Proposed fix (needs his decisions, one at a time): (1) share the *Black Bloc* 1Password vault with one Lead; (2) add that Lead as a GitHub collaborator, a Fly org member (means moving the app to a non-personal org), and a Discord application team member; (3) a GitHub Actions deploy on merge to main with a Fly deploy token (the parked "deploy button" idea) so a Lead can ship by merging; (4) make the test-mode lift reachable without Fly (a site switch behind Manage Server, or a documented flyctl step for the Lead); (5) `docs/access/if-the-owner-is-away.md` naming who holds what. Status: logged, waiting on the owner.

**Landing ritual (unchanged):** branch → `git merge --no-ff` on `main` → `scripts/deploy.ps1`
DETACHED (refuses a dirty tree; ~3 min now: ruff → pytest `-n auto` → ES-module parse of every
site asset → check.mjs → push → `flyctl deploy --app
black-bloc --ha=false --remote-only --yes`; flyctl lives at
`C:\Users\nbasl\AppData\Local\Microsoft\WinGet\Packages\Fly-io.flyctl_Microsoft.Winget.Source_8wekyb3d8bbwe\flyctl.exe`)
→ EDIT the skeleton line it appends to `docs/deploys.log` → verify the boot log
(`flyctl logs --app black-bloc --no-tail`) → move the 🔧 item WHOLE to `DONE.md`, flip the
design-doc header and the `info/README.md` row to SHIPPED, re-key `code-notes.md` → commit,
push → tell the owner what to click.

**Gotchas that cost time recently:** `node --check file.js` passes a broken ES module (parses
as CommonJS) — use `--input-type=module`; the site is a `StaticFiles(html=True)` mount so
page links carry `.html` (`logkinds.FEATURE_PAGES`); `flyctl ssh console -C` from
PowerShell splits on spaces — run one-offs from Bash with the script base64-encoded (see
`DONE.md` 2026-09-03 third pass); "The handle is invalid" after a flyctl ssh command is
noise, the command ran; a `discord.ui.View` replaced on a message is NOT stopped — call
`stop()` yourself or its timeout fires later against the wrong content
(`ui/view.py:940–968`); `commands synced` counts TOP-LEVEL commands — a `Group` is one slot,
so collapsing subcommands into a panel does not change it.

## 📋 Session 2026-10-06

- **Owner 2026-10-06, verbatim: "task for today, BaF only events when its going to be multiple runs back to back we will only ping marathon role once per event day 2 hours before the start of the run. everything else stays the same. Add a opt out for individual runner pings next to the highlight run button, that way some runners wont get pinged when they're getting into the zone for their run. We also want to revert the host vs runner pings opt in opt out change we made. Out host pings out by default opt runner pings on by default. then with each scanned run have an opt out/opt in override so some runners can be announced if staff allow runner to be annoucned at all. ask questions about this if needed before building. get all details"** Four parts: (1) BaF-only events with back-to-back runs: the Marathon role is pinged ONCE per event day, 2 hours before the first run; (2) a per-runner ping opt-out beside the Highlight run button; (3) revert the host-vs-runner opt-in/opt-out change - hosts OFF by default, runners ON by default; (4) a per-run opt-in/opt-out override on each scanned run. Status: **BUILDING** - five questions asked and answered one at a time (below).
  - **Q1 DECIDED 4:1x PM (owner, verbatim: "c and the schedule sheet should also mark if something is a BaF run. I think we should @leads if an event is say 75% BaF runs maybe? basically we should try and know and ping leads if we arent sure.")** - a BaF event is known BOTH ways: automatically when every run of the event day has a BaF runner, AND by a staff switch per marathon that forces it on or off. NEW from this answer: (i) the Marathon tracker (`/schedule.html`) marks each run that is a BaF run; (ii) when the bot is NOT sure - around 75% of the day's runs are BaF but not all - it @-mentions Leads and asks, rather than guessing. Conductor's defaults, each a settings key: 100% = BaF event without asking; from `75`% up to under 100% = ask Leads once per marathon, in the marathon's staff thread, with Yes / No buttons; under 75% = not a BaF event, no question.
  - **Owner 4:1x PM, verbatim: "yes to all of that but i was talking about the github pages schedule, that should be marking events as BaF right? like was today marked as BaF"** - the Q1 setup is confirmed, and "the schedule sheet" meant the ORGANISERS' sheet, not our tracker page. MEASURED 4:1x PM: yes - the Hotfix source names every run's show, and today's is **"Black in a Flash"** (marathon 11, name `Black in a Flash`, `source` gdq_hotfix, 4 runs, 4 ours). So the SHOW'S NAME is the strongest signal and comes first: a show whose name matches a settings-key list (default `Black in a Flash`) is a BaF event without asking; the 100% / 75% rule covers events on other sources and a renamed show. The tracker-page mark for BaF runs stays in scope (it costs little and he said yes to it).
  - **Q2 DECIDED 4:2x PM (owner, verbatim: "2. c. Right now we say @skyaiva is running Pokemon in 2 hours, thats the ping im talking about for the runners themselves.")** - NOT (a). The "ping" is the runner's own @-mention in the post. The per-runner opt-out (a button beside the run's highlight/opt-out button on each scanned run's post) makes the bot write that runner's NAME AS PLAIN TEXT, no @, in every public post about them on that marathon; the post itself, its timing and its role mentions are unchanged. Measured: every public marathon send is `AllowedMentions(users=False)` (`cogs/content/marathon_public.py:239`, `marathon.py:3088`), so by the code the @ is drawn but should not notify - told to the owner; if a runner really got a notification, that is a separate bug to chase.

CAUSE: the post body `pb_feed_post_text` ships as `{member} ran …`, and `{member}` is an @-mention (`<@id>`) placed INSIDE AN EMBED description - Discord does not reliably resolve user mentions inside embeds (raw `<@id>` or @unknown-user for many viewers, mobile especially); go-live avoids this by naming the member in the embed's author line. Not a matching fault: the right member was found. - 🔁 **GDQ's overnight REPLAY is still being spotlighted (owner, 2026-10-04 06:2x, verbatim: *"GDQ is doing a replay / Didn't we write code to not spotlight replays"*).** Yes — v182 (`golive_replay.py`, `golive_replay_action` = plain) — and it missed this. Three reasons, each enough, read in the code: (1) the verdict is taken when a session STARTS (`spotlight.py` ~881) and `_replay_seen` only handles a session already judged a replay — a live session that turns into a replay mid-broadcast is never downgraded, and GDQ rolls from show to replay in one broadcast (sessions 15, 16, 18 show the same shape on weeknights); (2) `verdict()` treats a title word as fuzzy and a live word overrules it — *tune in live* matches `golive_replay_live_words` = live; (3) a marathon on the channel within reach overrules a title word, and GDQueer's span covers the overnight gap. Twitch's own rerun type is not set by GDQ. Knock-on: Halo Infinite (GDQueer day 1's last run) still reads live on the tracker. Proposed to the owner, NOT built, waiting on his word: downgrade/upgrade mid-session; a bracketed tag at the start of the title (*[Replay]*) is certain, not fuzzy; the marathon exemption only while a run is scheduled around now; the marathon's live run is marked done when the replay starts; deploy after day 2. 🚀 **Owner 06:3x, verbatim: *"Build run now let's try and deploy before it starts"*** → 🔨 BUILD `replay-midstream` dispatched 06:3x (worktree `C:/lcw/bb-replay-midstream`); GO to deploy it as soon as it lands green and reviewed, BEFORE Sun 10:00 AM Phoenix (GDQueer day 2). - 🪪 **A typo fixed in the source sheet mid-show made the bot treat a LIVE run as a new one (found by the conductor, 2026-10-04 21:0x).** Measured through the operator token: at 03:43:04Z (8:43 PM) GDQ's sheet changed Metroid Dread's category from *Minim Items Glitchless* to *Minimum Items Glitchless*. A run's identity is built from its game and category text (`marathon_hotfix._runs`: `external_id` = `metroid-dread/minim-items-glitchless` → `…/minimum-items-glitchless`), so `marathon.schedule_changed added=1 dropped=1`: run 163 — LIVE since 03:19Z with champrul's host highlight up — was DROPPED, and run 176 was added and went live at 03:43Z. What members saw in #upcoming-events: champrul's first highlight edited to its finished wording 24 minutes into the run, a SECOND host highlight posted at 8:43 PM (`marathon.host_highlight_posted` run 176), and BOTH his heads-up posts (2 h and 15 min) edited to say the run is *off the schedule* (`host_reminder_edited dropped=True`). The run's start now reads 8:43 PM instead of 8:19 PM. At the v203 boot the first highlight was logged `host_highlight_lost` — the message was gone (deleted by hand, presumably; not confirmed). Not caused by v203 or by any of tonight's builds: the identity rule dates from v188. Fix to build (a persisted key — a migration to change, not an edit): when a schedule read drops a run and adds one in the same slot of the same day (same order and planned time, same runners), treat it as the SAME run renamed — keep its id, state, real start, posts and reminders. ➕ **Owner 21:0x, verbatim: *"yes make the change, also yes we did delete the duplicate post"*** → the deleted duplicate is CONFIRMED his (the `host_highlight_lost` row is explained); 🔨 BUILD `run-identity` dispatched 21:0x (worktree `C:/lcw/bb-run-identity`): a run dropped and a run added in the same slot of the same show-day with the same runners is the same run renamed. Deploy waits for the owner's word; an independent review before it ships. 🔨 **LANDED 21:2x** (branch last commit `beba74ed`; pairing in `marathon.py` `renames` / `same_slot` — planned starts within `marathon_move_minutes`, or same order within 12 h, plus an equal runner set; unambiguous pairs only; `rekey_runs` writes id changes in one transaction; no schema, no key, one log kind `marathon.run_renamed`; agent's gate 10271 passed + 3 skipped). 🔎 **INDEPENDENT REVIEW 21:4x: "deploy before tomorrow's show"**, with one NEW wrong-glue to close first: when a run is deleted and its same-runner neighbour retitled in ONE save, the retitled run inherits the deleted run's row (state, real start, sent marks, highlight) — and a tracked show has one runner with two consecutive runs of the same game (Game Masters, Fri 10-09: Ryan Ford, Donkey Kong Country 2 ×2). The builder was resumed 21:4x to add the rule *when more than one gone row shares the runner set, the texts must also be alike*. Confirmed sound by the review: `rekey_runs` is atomic and its parked id cannot collide; nothing but a display field reads `external_id` elsewhere; the incident's rows 163/176 are not touched by the first read; a rename re-arms nothing unless the start also moved. Left as follow-ups: a renamed run's Discord event is not retitled; of two identical game/category rows, deleting the FIRST still moves row 1 onto slot 2 and drops row 2 (pre-existing, a test asserts it); a live block's already-posted heads-ups keep the old spelling. ✅ **The glue case is closed and the build is MERGED to main `4032696a`, NOT DEPLOYED** (branch last commit `99140497`): when more than one lost row shares the fresh run's runner set, the normalised *game + category* must also be alike (equal, or difflib ≥ `SAME_WORDS` 0.93 — measured: the incident pair 0.973 passes; DKC2 102% vs True Ending 0.759 and vs Any% 0.88 fail) and exactly one may pass. Full gate on main after the merge: ruff clean, **10285 passed + 3 skipped**; keys 737, schema 86. Known and accepted for now: in the delete-P-and-retitle-Q read, Q is still dropped and Q′ added (the slot rule is not relaxed); two same-runner runs with IDENTICAL text both renamed in one read are no longer paired. ✅ **v204 LIVE Sun 21:59:48** (release `3e0aa81c`, `deploys.log` line 203; owner 21:5x, after pasting champrul's finished highlight — *champrul hosted Metroid Dread — Minimum Items Glitchless today on GDQueer · https://twitch.tv/gamesdonequick* — verbatim: *"thgis seems good. deploy when ready"*; gate green first run; boot clean; GDQ still a replay after the restart, no stray bump). ✅ **GDQueer ENDED 21:52** (operator read): `golive.replay_began` for session 20 two polls after the title turned to *[Replay] GDQueer Day 2 …*, unpinned, re-worded; `marathon.run_done` Metroid Dread `because=replay`; champrul's host highlight edited to its finished wording — SEEN BY THE OWNER in Discord, the first by-eye proof of v195's past-tense wording and v201's replay close. - 🎭 **A tone is a STARTING POINT, with Reroll and Pin as two moves (owner, 2026-10-05 11:5x, verbatim: *"I don't want these to be pins just starting points / Maybe we need a reroll and a pin option"*).** Said after the session rolled tones for nine members (PT and the Aunties / Uncles — DONE ▸ 2026-10-05) and could only set them as PINS, the one ahead-of-time move the bot has. How it works today, read in `chat_voice.py` `heard_from` 11:5x: under `chat_personality` = pool a member's tone is rolled FRESH for every conversation (a conversation = model turns inside a 30-minute window; the roll is seeded by guild, member and the window's start), drifts inside that conversation (every 4 turns, 25 % chance, one step along the tone graph), and nothing carries to the next conversation; a pin overrides all of it and never drifts. So no stored *starting point* exists. To build (after the decisions): a per-member starting tone the pool begins each conversation from; **Reroll** (a new random starting tone) and **Pin…** (fixed, no drift — today's move) on each row of Chat ▸ Who hears what and in `/chat` ▸ Who hears what…; the nine pins of 2026-10-05 converted to starting points. ❓ Decisions, asked one at a time: (1) does each new conversation begin again from the member's starting tone, or does the drift carry over so the tone wanders for good; (2) does a member with no starting tone get one written the first time the bot answers them, or keep a fresh roll per conversation; (3) a *roll for everyone in a role* move. Until it ships the nine pins stay as placeholders. Nothing dispatched. ➕ **Owner 11:5x, answering decision 1, verbatim: *"I want this to be stored in memory in the sense that it's not just 30 minutes of conversation but little snippets to the bot feel more responsive and real"*** → conductor's reading, told to him: the tone (and where it drifted to) is REMEMBERED between conversations instead of re-rolled every 30-minute window, and the bot carries small remembered snippets about each person from one conversation to the next. The second half already exists and is ON — measured 11:5x through the operator token: `chat_memory_mode` on, consent opt-out, 6 notes + 5 open threads + a name per member, 180 days, `/memory` for the member — but it has remembered NOTHING: `/api/chat/memory` total **0** profiles; the chat log since 09-15 holds **11** `chat.llm_reply` rows in all and ONE `chat.memory_distil_failed` (2026-09-24 01:32Z, cause not read — the Fly buffer does not reach it). So the build is two halves: (A) a stored, persistent tone per member with Reroll and Pin, and (B) finding why the one distillation failed and proving memory end to end on a real conversation. Decision 2 (write a tone at first answer) falls out of A; decision 3 (roll for a role) is taken as yes unless he objects. ❓ NEW decision asked: what the snippets may be — the facts a member told the bot, as today, or also *how we talk* (in-jokes, the member's manner, what was laughed about). ➕ **Owner 12:0x, verbatim: *"Facts and how we talk"* and *"I still want personality to be able to drift but less as time goes on. Basically if the responses match the tone we keep it, if the response is genuinely about how the bot was mean or wrong we adjust the tone."*** → DECIDED: (1) memory keeps facts AND rapport (*how we talk*), under the same caps, retention, `/memory` visibility and opt-out; (2) a member's tone is stored and carries between conversations; it still drifts, but the chance SHRINKS as conversations pass without complaint (the tone settles); (3) a member's reply that genuinely says the bot was mean or wrong moves the tone (and un-settles it); banter does not. 🔨 TWO BUILDS dispatched 12:0x, in parallel: `tone-settles` (worktree `C:/lcw/bb-tone-settles` — the stored tone, the settling drift, the feedback move, Reroll + Pin on both doors, roll for a role, the nine pins of today converted to starting tones) and `memory-rapport` (worktree `C:/lcw/bb-memory-rapport` — why the one distillation failed, rapport snippets beside the facts, an end-to-end proof that a snippet returns in the next conversation). Deploy waits for the owner's word; an independent review of each first. 🔨 **`memory-rapport` LANDED 14:2x — on its branch, NOT merged** (last commit `077812eb`; agent's gate 10349 passed + 3 skipped; no schema change; 3 keys — `chat_memory_min_turns` 1, `chat_memory_rapport_max` 4, `chat_memory_rapport_line`; registry 737 → 740). **Why memory had stored nothing — MEASURED cause 1:** the distiller borrowed the reply path's 400-token ceiling (`DISTIL_MAX_TOKENS = 600` was defined and passed to nothing) and the default model (`openai/gpt-oss-120b`, a reasoning model) spends that ceiling thinking — 2 of 24 real calls failed at 400 with *max completion tokens reached*, 0 of 8 at 1200; **cause 2, inferred:** almost nothing was ever offered — 11 model replies in 20 days, each needing a second message from the same member. Fixed: its own client at 1200; every write-up ends in a named outcome row (`chat.memory_sweep`; failures carry reason codes; counts only, never text). ⚠️ For the owner: (a) the agent made **36 real Groq calls** on the dev `.env` key (synthetic transcripts, $0) and drew four 429s around 2:00 PM — if the live bot shares that key its chat may have been rate-limited for a moment (no failed chat row is in the live log since 1:30 PM); (b) it CHANGED a default — one member message is now enough to be written up (was 2); (c) the write-up runs once every **24 hours** and at each boot, on conversations at least an hour old — the design doc said hourly — so a snippet cannot return sooner than the next day's sweep unless the cadence changes; (d) DMs never reach a model, so nothing is ever learned in a DM; (e) a failed write-up loses that conversation (KI-43 on the branch). Rapport rules as built: ≤120 chars, no quotes, no third persons, no instruction-shaped words, no links, no personal topics; re-checked on the way into every prompt. The new four-field prompt has NOT been run against the real model. An independent review (privacy, consent, prompt injection) was dispatched 14:2x. `tone-settles` still building. 🔎 **REVIEW BACK 14:3x: "deploy after fixes".** What bounds the risk (confirmed in code): only the SPEAKER's own profile ever enters a prompt, memory sits in the user turn, and nothing in the bot acts on a stored line — so a planted line can only change how the bot words replies to the person who planted it. Staff view, API payload and log rows carry counts and reason codes only — confirmed clean. To fix first (sent to the builder 14:3x): `chat_memory_rapport_max` = 0 did not stop stored lines entering prompts; a *Forget everything* during a write-up could be undone by it; the personal-topics rule covered rapport lines but not notes, and the sensitive lists missed health, sexuality, age, location, immigration, criminal history and money; the instruction filter was bypassed by a zero-width character, a look-alike letter or leetspeak; lines stored under older rules were not re-checked on the way out. Also asked for: the write-up cadence as a settings key (default stays 24 h until the owner decides — ❓ asked 14:2x, hourly recommended). ➕ **Owner 14:3x, verbatim: *"hourly"*** → DECIDED: the memory write-up runs every hour (the key's default is 1); told to the builder 14:3x. 🔨 **`tone-settles` LANDED 14:3x — on its branch, NOT merged** (last commit `4d8d7906`; all six parts incl. the feedback move; agent's gate 10445 passed + 3 skipped; **schema 86 → 87**; **31 keys**, registry 737 → 768; three new `/api/chat/voices` routes). As built: drift chance = max(floor, start × 0.5^(settled ÷ halves)) — 25 % → 12.5 % after 4 conversations → 6.25 % after 8 → the 2 % floor from 15, weighed every 4 model answers; a complaint is judged by ONE cheap model call, made only when a message after a model answer contains a cue word (28 defaults, a key) — the review tagger could not be reused, it never sees the follow-up; *mean* steps to the nearest enabled tone earlier in `chat_tone_gentle_order` (warm, cozy, shy, peppy, scholar, dramatic, flirty, noir, deadpan, mischievous, tsundere), *wrong* in `chat_tone_careful_order` (scholar, shy, warm, cozy, deadpan, noir, peppy, flirty, tsundere, mischievous, dramatic); pins never move; Reroll / Set tone… / Pin… / Unpin and Roll for a role… on both doors; the nine pins of 2026-10-05 become starting tones in the 86 → 87 migration (matched by member id + UTC day). ⚠️ UNPROVEN: whether the model really tells banter from a genuine complaint — every verdict in the tests is faked. An independent review was dispatched 14:4x. 🔎 **TONE REVIEW BACK 14:5x: "deploy after fixes".** Confirmed sound: the 86 → 87 migration (idempotent, one transaction, the nine ids exact, rerun-safe after a rollback), the drift maths, one member can never move another's tone, a planted verdict can do no more than one tone step. **The blocker:** the judge also read messages NOT addressed to the bot (any message in the same channel within 5 minutes of an answer that contains a cue word) and sent them to the model provider — a new class of member text leaving the server, with no opt-out. Sent back to the builder 14:5x: judge ONLY a reply to the bot's own answer or an @-mention, and delete the minutes key; check a pin before the model call; count the call against the member's own hourly fuse; stop drift returning to the tone just complained about; log a re-roll of a switched-off tone; a confirm, a full log and an undo on *Roll for a role… ▸ everyone* and no @everyone. ✅ **`memory-rapport` review fixes LANDED 14:4x** (branch `619e86c2`; 4 keys incl. `chat_memory_sweep_hours` default 1; agent's gate 10423 passed): the cap applies at render; *Forget* wins over a write-up in flight; ten private categories on notes, topics and rapport; lines are normalised before judging and storing; facts re-checked on the way out; only the write-up goes hourly, the knowledge re-read stays daily. A first TRIAL MERGE of both branches onto main (`C:/lcw/bb-chat-trial`) was green before the tone fixes: 772 keys, schema 87, 163 chat keys, ruff clean, 10583 passed + 3 skipped — to be redone when the tone fixes land. ✅ **TONE FIXES LANDED 15:1x** (branch `68cde5dc`; its gate 10478 passed): only a reply to the bot's own answer or an @-mention is ever judged (the same-channel path and `chat_tone_feedback_minutes` are gone); a pin is checked before the model call; the call counts against the member's own hourly fuse; a complaint eases settledness ONE halving back (not to zero) and keeps the complained-about tone out of that member's drift for 8 conversations; a re-roll of a switched-off tone is logged; *Roll for a role…* asks first with the count, refuses @everyone, logs every member and has **Undo this roll** (valid until the next roll, once). A complaint said to the bot is judged before the answer is worded, capped at 3 s. ✅ **BOTH BUILDS MERGED to main 15:1x, NOT DEPLOYED** (`9e1896eb`: `memory-rapport` then `tone-settles`; conflicts were the hand counts and `code-notes.md` only): **778** keys, schema **87**, 169 chat keys by import; ruff clean; **10616 passed + 3 skipped** on the merged tree. Review on the local mock (restarted from main): http://localhost:8797/chat.html — *Who hears what* (Reroll, Set tone…, Pin…, Roll for a role…) and *Memory* (the two chip rows). ⚠️ STILL UNPROVEN, only live use can show it: whether the model tells banter from a genuine complaint; the memory prompt with the rapport field against the real model; anything in Discord. ✅ **v205 LIVE Mon 15:23:29** (release `76cec233`, `deploys.log` line 204; owner 15:1x, verbatim: *"now"*; gate green first run; boot clean; the migration logged *9 pin(s) of 2026-10-05 are starting tones now*; 12 members hold a stored tone, none pinned; memory on, hourly, 0 profiles so far; today's show untouched). TO WATCH, the first days are the test: the first `chat.memory_sweep` row and whether a profile appears (a conversation must be an hour old, then the next hourly run); the first `chat.voice_feedback` / `chat.voice_feedback_held` / `chat.voice_drifted` rows; whether a judged complaint was banter or genuine by a human's reading; the daily turn fuse now having three consumers. Documented, not fixed: a line learned in one channel is used in every server channel (no output filter); the *staff talk* skip is a word test (a conversation mentioning ban / mod / report / auntie / uncle is never written up); older code would read rapport lines as plain notes on a rollback.

Measured 2026-09-23 at `ffa19473`: `git ls-files --eol | grep -c "i/crlf"` = **0** (nothing CRLF in the index), `git ls-files --eol | grep -c "w/crlf"` = **5** working-tree files — the three `scripts/*.ps1` (CRLF on purpose, per the attribute), `site/public/assets/release.json` and `docs/archive/current-bots/discord-scan-2026-08-26.md`. The writers: `scripts/release_json.py:36` uses `Path.write_text` (text mode → CRLF on Windows) and `scripts/deploy.ps1:106` uses `Add-Content` (CRLF). Remaining choice (owner's, cosmetic): leave it, or make those two writers emit LF (`write_text(..., newline="\n")`; build the `deploys.log` line with `[IO.File]::AppendAllText` and `` "`n" ``) and run one `git add --renormalize .` commit.
